39
National: More wealth, same divide
Net financial wealth is distributed less unevenly within
countries than across the world. In 2025, the richest 10%
owned an average of 60.9% of national net financial
assets across the 57 countries examined, using an
unweighted average, compared with 85.4% at the global
level. The gap between average and median wealth
was also considerably smaller: the national average-
to-median ratio was 3.12, again measured as an

unweighted average.
When it comes to inequality, however, the national
picture is particularly disappointing. Despite being a
major political issue for years, there has been virtually
no progress towards more even wealth distribution.
In 2005, the richest 10% held an average of 60.4% of
national wealth, while the average-to-median ratio
stood at 3.11. Two decades later, both measures are
almost unchanged. Wealth has grown substantially, but
its distribution within individual countries has remained

remarkably resistant to change.
This overall stability conceals sharper shifts at the
country level. In 32 of the 57 countries examined, the
share of the richest 10% changed by less than 2pps
between 2005 and 2025 (Figure 32). Where larger
shifts occurred, however, the balance was clearly tilted
towards greater inequality, with deterioration almost
twice as likely as improvement. Wealth concentration
increased in 16 countries and declined in only nine.

Among those recording an improvement, Cambodia
saw the largest decline in the top-decile share, followed
by Portugal and Slovakia. Latvia, Hungary, Belgium,
Ireland, Malaysia and the Netherlands also registered
meaningful decreases. Different starting points were at
work. Portugal, Latvia and Ireland moved down from
relatively high levels of concentration, while Slovakia,
Hungary and the Netherlands improved from already
more balanced positions. In the Netherlands, the richest

10% saw their share fall by 2.4pps to 56.1%.
At the other end of the spectrum, China recorded by far
the largest increase in concentration. The share of the
richest 10% rose by 15.7pps between 2005 and 2025 to
68.0%, well above the average national share of 60.9%.
This accompanied the enormous economic and social
transformation of the past two decades, which created
not only broad-based wealth but also a substantial
private upper class. China illustrates the difficulty of

combining rapid wealth creation with an equally rapid
diffusion of its benefits. After all, it was the unbridled
growth of the „wild“ 2000s, when the private sector
was subject to few restrictions, that primarily caused
inequality to rise. In sharp contrast, in the last five years,
the concentration of wealth has remained unchanged.

The ratio of average to median wealth provides an
important cross-check: it shows whether rising average
wealth is also reflected in the position of the typical

household. Across the 57 countries examined, the
average ratio remained virtually unchanged, rising from
3.11 in 2005 to 3.12 in 2025. This confirms the broader
picture of persistent national wealth inequality.
The strongest improvements occurred in the
Netherlands, where the ratio fell from 4.04 to 2.48, and
Ireland, where it declined from 4.87 to 3.42. In both
countries, the narrowing gap indicates that the middle
of the distribution gained ground relative to average

wealth.
The largest deteriorations were recorded in Russia,
Sweden and China. Russia’s ratio rose from 2.99 to 4.77,
while Sweden’s increased from 3.77 to 5.25. In China, it
climbed from 2.03 to 3.33. Median Chinese net financial
wealth increased substantially, but average wealth grew
faster. China thus illustrates a central tension of rapid
wealth creation: prosperity can spread while inequality
rises at the same time.
More broadly, the limited movement in both distribution

measures across most countries suggests that wealth
inequality is deeply entrenched. Two decades of
economic growth, crises and political attention have
barely altered the underlying distribution.

China was not alone. The top-decile share increased by
10.5pps in Russia and by 6.8pps in India, where rising

Concentration also rose in several advanced economies.

markets and with no improvement in sight. Sweden and
Switzerland also recorded increases of more than 4pps.
France saw a smaller rise of 3.1pps, although its top-
decile share of 54.8% remained below Germany’s 59.5%.
Rising concentration is therefore not confined to emerging
economies, nor does rapid development inevitably produce
it.

Appendix A: Methodological comments
General assumptions
The Allianz Global Wealth Report analyses gross financial assets held by households, i.e. cash and bank deposits,
receivables from insurance companies and pension institutions, securities (shares, bonds and investment funds) and other
receivables, as well as household liabilities. Net financial assets are defined as gross financial assets minus liabilities.
Real estate is excluded from this measure and analyzed separately where data are available. The report is based

on data from 57 countries. This group of countries covers 91% of global GDP and 72% of the global population. In 43
countries, we had access to statistics from the macroeconomic financial accounts. In the other countries, we were able to
estimate the volume of total financial assets based on information from household surveys, bank statistics, statistics on
assets held in equities and bonds and technical reserves.
To eliminate exchange-rate distortions over time, financial assets and liabilities denominated in national currencies are

converted into euros using exchange rates at the end of 2025. Unless explicitly described as real, these values are not
adjusted for inflation. The closing date for data to be included in the report is 31 July.
Statistical distinctions
The process associated with the introduction of the European System of Accounts 2010 (ESA 2010) in September 2014
involved updating and harmonizing the guidelines governing the preparation of many macroeconomic statistics. The

new requirements also apply to the macroeconomic financial accounts. One change relates to private households: under
the ESVG 2010 regulations, the two sectors „Private households“ and „Non-profit institutions serving households“ are
now reported separately. This also has implications for the Allianz Global Wealth Report, which takes data from the
macroeconomic financial accounts as a basis where available. For many countries, however – particularly those outside

of the EU – there is no separate data available for these sectors in general. So in order to ensure global comparability,
this publication analyzes both sectors together under the heading „private households“.
Determination of wealth bands for global wealth classes
Lower wealth threshold: There is a close link between financial assets and the incomes of private households. According
to Davies et al. (2009) , private individuals with below-average income tend to have no assets at all, or only very few. It is

only when individuals move into middle and higher income groups that they start to accumulate substantial assets.
We have applied this link to our analysis. Countries in the upper-middle income bracket (based on the World Bank‘s
country classification system) therefore form the group in which the average household assets first reach a meaningful
level. This value marks the lower threshold for the global middle wealth class.
In terms of income, households with incomes that correspond to between 75% and 150% of average net income are

generally considered to constitute the middle class. According to Davies et al., households with income corresponding
to 75% of the average income have assets that correspond to 30% of the average assets. As far as the upper threshold
is concerned, 150% of average income corresponds to 180% of average assets. Consequently, we have set the threshold
values for the wealth middle class at 30% and 180% of average net financial assets per capital. In 2025, average net

financial assets per capita in the countries covered stood at approximately EUR36,690. Applying these percentages gives
the rounded thresholds used in the workbook: EUR11,000 and EUR66,000. Individuals with net financial assets between
these thresholds belong to the global middle-wealth class. Those below the lower threshold belong to the low-wealth
class, while those above the upper threshold belong to the high-wealth class.
Appendices
14  Davies, James B. et al. (2009), The level and distribution of global household wealth, NBER working paper 15508.

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX tăng điểm trong tuần qua, 20.45 điểm tương ứng 1.14%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Tiện ích khí là các nhóm ngành có mức tăng rõ nét nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 2,680 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, HDB và BSR; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở 
mức 1,172 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại mua ròng 3,009 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 5,001 hợp đồng. Tự doanh bán ròng -9,259 hợp đồng và đang duy 
trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng 9,458 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần tăng điểm tích cực, chỉ số VNINDEX chinh phục thành công 
ngưỡng tâm lý 1.800 điểm với thanh khoản cải thiện, độ rộng thị trường nghiêng về số mã 
tăng điểm và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài quay trở lại mua ròng. Động lực hỗ trợ thị trường 
trong tuần qua đến từ hoạt động cơ cấu của các quỹ bám theo rổ chỉ số mới nổi của FTSE, 
khi mà ngày 21/9 là ngày Việt Nam được chính thức nâng hạng. Thêm vào đó, diễn biến hạ 
nhiệt của giá dầu khi lo ngại thiếu hụt cung do xung đột ở Trung Đông giảm bớt cũng là 
yếu tố hỗ trợ tâm lý thị trường chung. Ở chiều ngược lại, thông tin FED tăng lãi suất có tác 
động tiêu cực nhưng ảnh hưởng không đáng kể lên thị trường do động thái này đã được 
dự báo từ trước.

### Page 3

3

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

### Page 4

4

Kết quả kinh doanh 2Q2026 tích cực 
nhờ hưởng lợi từ căng thẳng địa 
chính trị ở Trung Đông

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA để định giá cổ phiếu GAS, chúng tôi đưa 
ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động 
mạnh

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

Tương quan mặt bằng giá  Diễn biến P/E của doanh nghiệp

NTP đang được giao dịch với mức P/E khoảng 
8,3 lần thấp hơn trung bình 5 năm của cổ 
phiếu. Với kỳ vọng doanh nghiệp sẽ bước vào 
pha tăng trưởng mạnh trong 3 năm tới, chúng 
tôi cho rằng P/E fw 2026F ở mức 9,5x lần phù 
hợp với mức dự phóng tăng trưởng 11,6% 
CAGR giai đoạn 2026 – 2028.

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ

NIM chịu áp lực trước mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng

Chất lư ợng tài s ản suy gi ảm nh ẹ do 
mặt bằng lãi suất ở mức cao

Khuyến ngh ị: MUA – Giá m ục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các y ếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 
VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ. 
Kháng cự xa: 1900 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1850 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1770 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1715 (+-15)

## Document 249: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-11
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova
Date:    8 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: French Toast & Brazilian Bossa Nova

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL:
French Toast & Brazilian Bossa
Nova
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 10 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Insaanu Studio / Unsplash

I. Bonds
The global bond market remains jittery, though yields may be starting to
stabilize after their rout in recent months (chart). Nevertheless, geopolitical
turbulence may still buffet them.

### Page 2

Yields have risen for an assortment of reasons, from better-than-expected
economic growth (US) to fiscal instability concerns (France). Brazil and
China are the only major countries showing declines in bond yields so far
this year (chart).
Bond yields have also risen on higher-for-longer oil prices as the war in
the Middle East has continued to disrupt oil supplies. The Houthis have
said airliners should avoid Saudi airspace, declaring it a zone of military

operations after the Saudi-backed Yemeni government launched an
offensive  against the group earlier this month. On Saturday, a third attack
this week on Riyadh's international airport left 12 people dead and dozens
wounded. President Donald Trump said the US will "look at" joining Saudi
strikes on Houthis.

### Page 3

II. Stay Home vs Go Global
So far this year, the US has held onto its 65% market-cap share of the All
Country World (ACW) MSCI (chart). Japan's share has also held steady at
5%. However, Europe's share has slipped (13.3%) to the advantage of the
share of the Emerging Markets (12.0%).
Nevertheless, last week, the US outperformed the Developed World ex-
US as the French debt crisis weighed on European stock prices (chart).
So far this month, the US MSCI is the second-best-performing stock price

index we track (chart). Brazil was the best-performing in response to first-
round presidential election results showing conservative Flavio Bolsonaro,
son of former President  Jair Bolsonaro, exceeding expectations in the
race against leftist President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva.

### Page 4

III. Earnings & Valuation
The ratio of forward earnings for the US MSCI to the ACW ex US MSCI
fell through the summer this year (chart). It stopped doing so in recent
weeks.
Forward P/Es have been falling worldwide since the start of the year, as
forward earnings have outpaced stock price gains (chart).

### Page 5

IV. Currencies
The French debt crisis is also weighing on the euro, which is down from
$1.20 at the start of this year to $1.12 (chart).
As a result, the DXY dollar index has rebounded to its best reading since
early 2025 (chart). We remain constructive on the US dollar.

### Page 6

V. Notable
(1) German factory orders fell sharply in August, plunging 10.6% m/m
(chart). The primary driver behind the contraction was a steep pullback in
large-scale contracts. The headline drop was almost entirely due to a
61.5% slump in large-scale orders for aircraft, ships, trains, and military
vehicles, which suffered a major correction following an exceptional surge
in July. When these volatile large-scale contracts are excluded, new
manufacturing orders actually slipped by just 0.1%, indicating that core

industrial demand was essentially treading water rather than experiencing
a broad-based collapse.
(2) CQQQ continues to underperform QQQ (chart). While China's long-
term industrial policy goals are ambitious, cyclical demand inside China

### Page 7

has struggled to generate the same explosive revenue growth seen in
Western tech sectors. Furthermore, China's tech industries carry a
persistent regulatory discount. Chinese technology sectors have
historically been vulnerable to abrupt policy shifts, anti-monopoly
crackdowns, data security reviews, and ongoing geopolitical tensions
(including US–China trade restrictions and semiconductor export
controls), which compress valuations and deter long-term institutional

clause.

### Page 8

Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

## Document 255: India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ Degrees of rupee defence (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

GROWTH MOMENTUM TO SUSTAIN, 
EXTERNAL RISKS LINGER

India registered  strong growth at the start of 
FY27. Sustaining this performance will depend 
increasingly on how effectively it navigates a 
more uncertain global environment. Besides 
trade fragmentation, geopolitical tensions 
leading to higher energy prices, and shifts in 
international capital flows present key external 
risks.

India joined its Southeast Asian peers in 
posting an upside surprise on growth in 
1QFY27 (Apr -Jun26), up a firm 7.8%  yoy. few 
points of note:  
A) strong 1Q growth print reinforced the view 
that India’s domestic demand cycle was more 
resilient than initially assumed, with 
consumption, public capex and manufacturing 
providing a solid base despite external shocks.  
B) prevailing tailwinds by way of indirect tax 
relief in 4Q25, real rate buffer, and easier 
monetary conditions (earlier cuts), were also 
supportive;  
C) nominal GDP growth returned above 10% 
after two years on higher deflators, though 
some segments like manufacturing faced 
negative deflators as input prices rose at a 
faster pace than output due to commodity price 
increases and import intensity;  
D) Revisions to backdated numbers, to account 
for output PPI series, pushed up FY26 growth to 
7.8% (from 7.7% earlier). Two prior years were 
also revised.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• High frequency indicators 
might keep  2QFY growth 
close to 7%. 
• Pace to moderate in 2HFY. 
• Retail inflation is likely to 
continue ris e and broaden  in 
scope, necessitating a tighter 
policy bias.  WPI likely to r ise 
by double-digits. 
• Special swap windows helped 
to stem bearish INR 
positioning. 
• Organic capital flows need to 
catch up.  
• Liquidity management is in 
focus, with a moderate 
correlation to inflation. 
• Twin-deficit risks are partly 
offset by stronger macro 
buffers.

High-frequency indicators

Into July -August, high frequency 
indicators are holding up, including GST 
e-way bills, indirect tax  revenues, 
electricity demand and growth in digital 
payments, while toll collections and 
petroleum consumption slipped . For 
demand, a contained extent of increase 
in retail pump prices shielded 
households from the surge in global 
crude prices, but other market linked 
fuel variants have climbed in recent 
months. On the employment front, job 
reductions were witnessed in the 
software sector, even as fresh 
opportunities were reported in global 
capability centres , electr onics/ 
semiconductors, healthcare, auto/ 
ancillary etc.  Wage growth in services 
has improved , manufacturing has been 
steady, while farm sector moderated, 
according t o consumption surveys by 
CMIE.

Industry indicators are a mixed bag, with 
PMIs maintaining their foothold in the 
expansionary terrain, while production 
and eight core index growth come off 
highs. Auto production continues to 
expand, though output and sales might 
moderate in the next quarter on base 
effects due to indirect tax cuts in the 
same time year ago. Forward-looking 
firm surveys have improved, after 
initially coming under pressure.

Sectors associated with rural demand  
might moderate amid weaker kharif 
sowing activity and lower reservoir 
levels impacting the rabi crop activity. 
Cumulative southwest monsoon is ~15% 
below the long-term average by tail end 
of the season. This takes the country on 
course to face its driest monsoon since 
2009 (which was 18% below LPA). 
Uneven geographical spread is notable, 
with south and east India registering 
nearly 25% shortfall.  While k harif 
sowing is on track with 99% of acreage 
completed, the winter crop might come 
under pressure as weather 
developments suggest El Niño  could 
extend into 2027.

### Page 3

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

With nominal GDP growth to track 11-12% 
this fiscal yea r, corporate earning s 
growth is expected to fare better than 
recent few years. 1QFY27 performance 
had already set off the year on a strong 
note, barring selected sectors like O&G 
which faced sequential marginal 
pressures from operating costs. Add to 
this, the centre’s capex gr owth is in firm 
double-digits, also backed by growth in 
core imports (non-oil non-gold).

On the external sector , despite better 
performance by exports, oil and 
electronics purchases have weighed on 
the goods math. The trade deficit is set 
to stay wide amidst elevated global 
crude cost, higher industrial fuels, 
cooking gas etc,  even as a wider mix of 
suppliers have addressed energy 
shortages. Notably, the net electronics 
deficit has widened considerably, just 
as exports have improved. Likewise, core 
imports (ex oil ex gold) have stayed firm, 
which partly feeds into higher exports as 
well as broader domestic demand. 
Monthly service trade surplus has been 
rangebound around $17 -18bn in recent 
months dwarfed by $25 -$30bn goods 
gap.

Maintaining growth forecasts

High frequency indicators put 2QFY 
growth close to 7%. We expect full year 
FY27 growth to average 7.3% yoy  from 
a revised 7.8% in FY26 , assuming 
moderation in the momentum in the 
second half of the year on tighter policy 
conditions, lagged impact of high energy 
prices and base effects from indirect tax 
cuts last year fade.  Assuming some 
spillover impact of exogenous 
uncertainties and tight financial 
conditions at home, we expect FY28 
growth to average 6.8-7.0%.

INFLATION OFF ITS BACK – 
THREE CATALYSTS

After two years of benign inflation, 
India’s price trend is off its back this 
year, mainly on the back of three

### Page 4

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

catalysts: Food, energy and early signs 
of demand pass through.

Aug inflation rose to a 20 -month high of 
4.8% yoy from 4.5% month before . As 
the chart highlights, number of sub -
segments growing by faster than the 
inflation target at 4% have continued to 
climb, pointing to a broadening out in the 
momentum.

Food inflation is gradually becoming 
more broad -based, shifting beyond 
vegetables and pulses to sugar, milk, 
protein groups, edible oils, amongst 
others. Sub-par weather has contributed 
to this upmove, besides idiosyncratic 
factors like swing in production  trends, 
diversion for fuel usage etc. After two 
years of normal monsoon, cumulative 
southwest rainfall this year is currently 
15% below the long -term average, 
alongside slower build -up in reservoir 
levels, which could impact upcoming rabi 
crops as well.  El-Nino patterns continue 
to strengthen, adding to seasonal swings 
and risk of impacting farm output.

Beyond food, g lobal oil prices have 
averaged ~$91pb in CY26, staying above 
$100pb for much of Seo26 . Retail fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
back in May26 and have been left 
unchanged since then , while CNG, 
aviation fuel, LPG etc have  continued to 
climb.

Transport inflation jumped to 4.6% yoy in 
Aug vs 0.1% in Jan26, besides utilities’ 
component inching up. Business price 
expectation surveys (conducted by IIM 
Ahmedabad) have, nonetheless, 
captured businesses facing a notable 
increase in fuel costs, especially 
industrial fuels, base metals and 
derivative product groups. Imported 
price pressures are most notabl e in the 
producer price/ wholesale price indices. 
These gauges have been hovering at 9 -
10% yoy in recent months.

### Page 5

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

Lastly, core inflation  (ex-food and fuel)  
rose to 1.5-month high in August, ma inly 
due to a sequential rebound in precious 
metals – gold and silver jewellery. 
Excluding precious metals, the reading 
was more moderate at 2.9%, pointing to 
some extent of economic slack. 
Nonetheless, other pockets like telecom, 
restaurants, business services, transport 
fares etc have been inching up, 
reflecting early signs of broadening in 
price pressures (on sequential basis), 
warranting attention. These are set 
against the backdrop of strong bank 
credit growth, flush INR liquidity and 
resilience in headline growth.

Our correlation analysis between 
liquidity (as % of NDTL) and core inflation 
– see chart, points to a positive but 
modest corelation at 0.4 between the 
two variables, with an assumed year -
worth lag on inflation. The inflation -
fighting mandate will also pus h the 
central bank to reduce the surplus 
liquidity in the domestic system.

A sh arp in global commodity prices is 
expected to fe ed into the wholesale 
price gauge , pushing up reading s to 
double-digits next quarter.

A gradual broadening of price 
pressures is likely to keep headline 
inflation above 5% in second half of the 
fiscal year , sharply narrowing the real 
rate buffer, underscoring the need for a 
tighter policy bias. Add to this, recent 
developments, including a sustained rise 
in crude prices, tightening global 
financial conditions, firm domestic 
growth and signs of broadening in core 
pressures, strengthen the case for a  
shallow 50bp hike in second half of FY27, 
making October’s meeting a live one.

TACKLING LIQUIDITY DELUGE, 
HIGHER YIELDS

Beating street estimates by a wide 
margin, the RBI’s special swap windows 
raised a cumulative $ 143bn into Sep , 
with the highest contribution (~92%) by

### Page 6

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

FCNR(B) deposits at $1 33bn, while rest 
were via offshore borrowing facilities. 
These flows pushed up the foreign 
reserves stock to a fresh high past 
$780bn, providing considerable 
firepower to defend the currency. This is 
already noticeable in the recent shift in 
the intervention bias. USDINR, which was 
earlier stubbornly steady in the face of 
strong FCNR inflows but  eased sharply 
due to strong intervention dollar sales 
and broader dollar swings, before being 
led higher due to external triggers.

Liquidity is a key watch factor. Given the 
swap arrangement, these inflows have 
added to already abundant INR liquidity 
backdrop, which was at a four year high, 
depressing overnight rates. The banking 
system surplus jumped past INR 10trn 
surpassing 2022 and Covid highs, with 
the durable balance widening sharply 
higher to INR 14trn, suppressing 
overnight call rates. This compares to 
average INR 1trn surplus in late-June.

While organic drivers like tax -related 
outflows, and seasonal currency leakage, 
in addition to CAD, portfolio outflows, 
and maturity of the forwards book will 
act as counter -balancing factors, yet 
concerted steps were required to drain 
the potential surge in liquidity.

We discussed the various seasonal, 
near-term and permanent measures  
that the central bank might consider (see 
FCNR bonanza necessitates deft 
management) to absorb this surfeit. 
Policymakers are being mindful of both 
the liquidity and signalling effects of 
the sterilisation tools at their disposal. 
The RBI utilised tranches of market 
operations like VRRR across tenors, 
after which three tranches of open 
market operations (bonds maturing FY29 
and FY32) worth a cumulative INR 1trn 
were conducted. These announcements 
fuelled a rise in yields, which were 
already inching up, tracking global 
counterparts. Sell/buy FX swaps for  
shorter rollovers were also being 
reportedly tapped.

When the dust settles, focus will also 
be on the bunched -up maturities that 
will fall due in 3Y and 5Y tenor of the 
deposits. A portion of the existing 
reserve stock could be earmarked 
against these liabilities, helping to 
mitigate concerns that deposit 
maturities or debt repayments could 
trigger a sharp increase in dollar demand 
and exert pressure on the FX market 
down the line. In the near-term, priorities 
will be to manage liquidity, gradually 
lower the sizeable forwards book, and 
support the domestic currency. Long -
end yields continue to be influenced by 
the hardening global yields.

BALANCE OF PAYMENTS MATH 
TO BE COMFORTABLE

The FY27 current account math is 
expected to display two key counter 
trends, Firstly, the wider goods trade 
imbalance will raise the FY27 current 
account deficit. Secondly, the 
counterbalancing effect of a sharp 
improvement in the capital flows math 
will keep the annual balance of 
payments math in surplus.

### Page 7

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            7

India recorded a current account deficit 
(CAD) of $10.5bn during April-July 2026, 
compared with $6.1bn in the 
corresponding period last year. The 
deterioration was driven almost entirely 
by a wider merchandise trade deficit, 
which rose to $117.8bn from $97.1bn a 
year ago. Part of this offset by higher  
income, transfers and capital account 
flow. Capital flows found support from 
net FDI, portfolio investments and 
banking capital, especially NRI deposits 
(jumped sharply to $36bn vs $4.6bn 
same period last year) , pushing up Apr -
Jul26 BOP to $12.7bn surplus, more than 
double of $4.8bn same time last year.

Oil prices are the main swing factor for 
the CAD this year , with the current run -
rate tracking our forecast at -1.1% of GDP 
for FY27. This suggests that the 
widening in the CAD is commodity -price 
related rather than driven by higher 
demand.

On the other hand, capital flows are 
being buttressed by the special swap 
windows introduced by the central bank, 
which raised a cumulative $ 143bn 
between June and August, through non -
resident deposits and external 
borrowings. This has helped to fil l the 
gap from subdued portfolio flows and 
net investment trends. We expect FY27 
current account deficit to track -1.1% of 
GDP in FY27, with the balance of 
payments to register upwards of $80bn 
surplus (vs -$23.6bn deficit last year).  
Into FY28, the dependency of capital 
flows on o rganic drivers like portfolio 
flows and FDI is set to increase, 
underscoring the need for higher 
vigilance.

FISCAL RUN-RATE

The FY27 fiscal run -rate points to a 
stronger revenue momentum, which 
has helped to keep the FYTD deficit in 
check, even as expenditure was 
frontloaded owing to subsidy payments 
and capex disbursements. April-July 
2026 deficit stands at a third of the full-
year balance.

### Page 8

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            8

Both tax and non -tax receipts fared 
better than the comparable period year 
ago, helped by stronger corporate tax 
collections, STT transaction revenues, 
and RBI dividend, amongst others.

Setting 2QFY for a strong start, net 
direct tax collections r ose 13% yoy by 
mid-Sep26, driven by net corporate tax 
collections up 19.5%. Net non -corporate 
tax collections were up 6%, besides STT 
collections up a sharp 53% yoy. Add to 
this, divestments have also fared better 
than expected this year, with ~78% 
raised by August, accompanied by 
dividends & profits (at 83% of budget by 
Jul26).

Under expenditure, about 33% of the full 
year allocation was allocated by July 
2026. The subsidy outgo rose sharply, up 
35% yoy, led by higher food at 23% and 
fertiliser at 46% (already accounting for 
nearly two-third of the full year budget)  
led by surging global DAP and urea 
prices. With global prices remaining high, 
the actual outgo might be higher than 
budgeted INR 1.7trn , likely towards INR 
2.5trn. Capital spending also remained 
apace, up 30% yoy in first five months of 
the year.

Overall, strong growth in tax and non-tax 
receipts allowed the centre to sustain 
higher spending while containing the 
extent of fiscal slippage until July 2026 
(FYTD). A strong nominal GDP rate 
(helped by higher inflation) will also 
provide a favourable denominator effect.

IN SUM

The economy exhibited resilience in the 
face of a tough external environment in 
the first quarter, with part of that 
stability likely to extend to 2QFY27 as 
well. Tighter monetary conditions, El 
Nino impact and base effects from 
passage of post -indirect tax jump will 
moderate headline growth in 2HFY. 
Nonetheless for the year, we expected 
growth to stay north of 7%, factoring in a 
strong start.

## Document 343: Webinar_ Europe’s investment landscape - what’s changing and why it matters

Source PDF: all report pdf/Webinar_ Europe’s investment landscape - what’s changing and why it matters.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Join ING economists to explore new research that challenges
perceptions of Europe's investment culture. We'll debunk some
common misconceptions, examine how attitudes and
behaviours are changing, and discuss what it all means for
Europe's future growth. Sign up here
Marieke Blom, Carsten Brzeski, Charlotte de Montpellier, Amrita
Naik Nimbalkar, Sebastian Franke, Paolo Pizzoli, Valentin Tataru
and Rebecca Byrne
Europe is often seen as a continent of cautious savers. But the story

is more nuanced than commonly believed. New ING research
suggests the gap between European and US savings is smaller than
often assumed, while attitudes towards investing may be shifting.
Half of Europeans with savings already invest, and another 30%
say they would consider doing so.
In this webinar, we'll discuss why this matters for household wealth
and economic growth, what is preventing more Europeans from
investing, and how attitudes diﬀer across countries. We'll also

explore what could help to unlock Europe's investment potential.
Click here to sign up.

## Document 345: Westpac Card Tracker, 2 October 2026 (2026-10-02)

WESTPAC–DATAX

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.
“... both the monthly
and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the quarterly
national accounts. All estimates are
adjusted for regular seasonal variations.
Quarterly growth momentum is now

tracking around 1.7%, the strongest pace
since the fuel price spike in March, and
pior to that, since the turn of the year.
• Notably, growth ex fuel has slowed
since the start of September, tracking a
slower 1.4% quarterly pace in the latest
week. This reflects both the end of the
temporary halving in fuel excise tax and
the latest resurgence in energy prices
globally.
• Recall that Q2 national accounts showed
nominal spending (excluding housing

costs, which are not covered in our card
tracker indicators) rose 0.9%qtr, similar
to Q1’s 1%qtr gain but with annual
growth slowing to 4.7%yr from 5.5%yr.
Growth in real, inflation-adjusted terms
was more subdued at +0.4%qtr, 1.8%yr
(near flat in per capita terms). The detail
was ‘messy’ with significant disruptions
from the Middle East conflict (see here).
• The card tracker data now has 12 of the
13 weeks of Q3. As such, the 1.7%qtr
rise in nominal terms and price rise in the

1%qtr range points to a real, inflation
adjusted gain of around 0.7%qtr, in line
with Westpac’s current forecasts.
Card activity points to ~0.7%qtr gain in Q3 in real, inflation adjusted terms

• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in card
activity across broad categories. The year
has been marked by rotations between
essentials and discretionary categories
as fuel price shocks have washed in
and out. The switch from essentials to
discretionary categories – services in

particular – in the early part of Q3 is again
reversing with the main drag coming in
discretionary goods.
• In quarterly growth terms, essential
goods is now leading, running at 2.5%qtr,
with discretionary services still relatively
strong at 2.2%qtr, essential services
tracking more slowly at 1.4%qtr and
discretionary goods much weaker at just
0.3%qtr (flat to down in per capita terms).
• Digging into the more detailed
discretionary services segments, the

initial quarterly pickup came from a big
turnaround in transport services, which
look to be partly a rebound from air travel
disruptions in Q2 with a similar pattern
evident in ‘international transactions’
(which are not included in discretionary
services category, as they include some
goods purchases, but are presented here
as corroborating evidence). The charts
on p6-7 provide some more detail on the
other categories. Notably the weakening
in discretionary goods momentum looks

to be broad-based.
• Regionally, the softening is a little more
evident in Vic than in other states.

• Our MHSI and retail card indexes 
are composite measures based on 
transactions in categories that are in

MHSI index measures significantly 
outperformed the broader WCTI in 
Q2 but with the gap narrowing in July-

show both more in line at 1.6%qtr and 
1.7%qtr respectively. 
• The retail card index measure is a 
narrower subset of spend that excludes 
fuel and most services except hospitality.

non food retail in recent weeks with 
food retail also stalling but hospitality 
tracking a firmer, albeit slowing pace.

the 3mth growth rate ticking up to

quarterly strength is coming from vehicle 
sales which surged through the middle of

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《公債利率攀升，信用市場波動放大》
近期隨著公債利率攀升 、利率波動放大 ，成熟市
場債市信用利差皆有所擴大 ，惟目前利差水準距
上半年貿易戰擔憂時期高點仍有一段距離；不過
現階段貨幣政策不確定性仍將持續使利率波動加
大、市場重新定價貨幣政策預期 ，在今年剩餘的
幾個月中信用利差仍有進一步擴大可能；另一方
面預期市場將衡量債券發行人抵禦高利環境的能
力，故發行人獲利能力以及現金流的強弱或將
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望

歐洲投資級債指數 正向 / 4.20 長期投資人可考慮鎖利取得豐沛孳息

美國非投資級債指數 正向/ 7.65 信評BB級以上之中大型企業債較為穩健
歐洲非投資級債指數 中性 / 6.10 違約率中期有望緩解，以現金流穩健發行人為主

重點摘要 左右市場表現 ，這將體現在高低評等發行人不僅基
本面的分化在高利環境下將更明顯 ，價格表現也將
更分歧。
《非投資級債信用基本面大致穩健，但分歧加大》
第二季美非投資級債發行人獲利狀況強勁 ，除交通
運輸、住房、電信和有線電視產業外 ，所有產業的
EBITDA(息稅折舊攤銷前淨利 )皆有成長，債務槓桿
出現六季以來首次下降 ，利息覆蓋率也提升至兩年
來的最高水準 ，但槓桿率和利息覆蓋率仍分別比指
數長期平均高出 0.17倍以及低 0.26倍，主要原因為
總債務成長 ，以及高利率環境下再融資成本更高 ，
令利息成本上升 。此外美非投資級債發行人過去 12
個月的資本支出年增幅創2023年第三季以來的最高
水準，主要由科技業帶動；不過在整體穩健的資產

負債表狀況中仍有 K型分化的情形 ，如43%的發行
人利息覆蓋率超過五倍 ，此比率為兩年新高 ，但同
時有21%的發行人利息覆蓋率低於 2倍，這也創了
近五年新高 ，反映在高利環境下債信基本面分化加
大，雖然目前違約狀況持穩 ，整體美非投資級債發
行人債信基本面在獲利支持下仍穩健 ，不過在利率
將在高檔區間維持一段時間的預期下 ，基本面的分
化有機會進一步加大 ，此外觀察過去歷史數據顯示
利息保障倍數與利率環境密切相關 ，如美歐信用債
利息保障倍數雖在2022年疫後復甦時期創近年高點 ，
但隨後又隨著利率走高而回落 ，因此儘管近期如上
述利息保障倍數出現小幅改善跡象 ，但基於美歐公
債利率的上升將增加債券再融資帶來的償債成本壓
力，預期債信指標未來也將承壓。
2
• 違約狀況持穩 ，美非投資級
債發行人債信基本面在獲利
支持下仍穩健 ，在利率將在
高檔區間維持一段時間的預
期下 ，基本面的分化有機會
進一步加大

整體而言在高利環境持續下非投資級債選券的重要

數已在一倍以下 ，反映利息償付能力不足 ，為成熟

2． 結果の詳細：直近は 10月 1日の公共料金引き上げが物価を大きく押し上げ 
9 月のロシアのインフレ率は 6.35％となり、8月（6.33％）からごくわずかに上昇、市場予想
（6.30％）から上振れた。ロシア中銀による季調済前月比の年率換算は6.5％となり、8月（7.5％）
から減速した。 
前年比インフレ率を大分類別に見ると、9月は食料品が 5.05％（前月：5.08％）、財（非食料品）
が 6.47％（前月：6.51％）、サービスが 7.97％（前月：7.85％）となり、食料品と財（非食料品）
でやや低下、サービスでやや上昇した。

## Document 457: 企業物価は鈍化も、上昇圧力はなお強い（26年9月企業物価指数） ～川下への価格転嫁が続く。資源高による再加速リスクも～

Source PDF: all report pdf/企業物価は鈍化も、上昇圧力はなお強い（26年9月企業物価指数） ～川下への価格転嫁が続く。資源高による再加速リスクも～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 13 日

1/4 
企業物価は鈍化も、上昇圧力はなお強い（26 年 9 月企業物価指数） 
～川下への価格転嫁が続く。資源高による再加速リスクも～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ ９月の国内企業物価指数は前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％（８月：前年比＋7.6％）。 前年比
上昇率は４ヵ月ぶりに＋７％を下回ったが、農林水産物や石油製品の下落による影響が大き
く、企業段階の物価上昇圧力はなお根強い。 
○ 川上・川中では価格上昇に一服感がみられる一方、川下のプラスチック製品では高い伸びが
続いている。既往のコスト高の価格転嫁はなお進んでおり、川下への物価上昇圧力は依然と
して強い。 
○ ＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体や銅などの価格上昇も目立つ。資源価格とは異なる経
路からの物価上昇圧力として注目される。 
○ 先行きは、川下への価格転嫁が続くなか、資源価格の高止まりによって川上からの上昇圧力
が再び強まるリスクがある。企業物価は当面高止まりし、消費者物価についても年度後半に
かけて上昇率を明確に高める可能性が高い。

1. 前年比は＋６％台に鈍化も、物価上昇圧力はなお根強い 
日本銀行から公表された2026 年９月の国内企業物価指数は、前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％と
なった。前月比では２ヵ月連続で下落し、前年比でも４ヵ月ぶりに＋７％を下回った。８月の前
年比＋7.6％から大きく鈍化しており、春先の急激な物価上昇には一服感がみられる。 
もっとも、企業段階での物価上昇圧力が大きく和らいだわけではない。今月は農林水産物や石
油・石炭製品の下落が全体の前月比を押し下げた一方、プラスチック製品など川下への価格転嫁
は続き、ＡＩ関連の価格上昇も目立った。また、足元の資源価格の高止まりを踏まえると、先行
きは川上からの上昇圧力が再び強まるリスクもあることにも注意が必要だ。 
前月比の内訳をみると、農林水産物が同▲8.8％（寄与度：▲0.42％ポイント）と大幅に下落し
た。玄米や精米、豚肉などが主な下落要因となった。米については、2026 年産米の概算金の引き
下げなどを背景に価格が大きく低下したとみられる。農林水産物は前年比でも▲13.3％と大幅な
マイナスとなっており、９月の企業物価を押し下げる大きな要因となった。

### Page 2

2/4 
また、石油・石炭製品も前月比▲2.2％と下落した（寄与度：▲0.15％ポイント） 。重油、軽
油、ジェット燃料油などの下落が影響している。農林水産物と石油・石炭製品のマイナス寄与は
合計▲0.57％ポイントと、国内企業物価全体の前月比▲0.3％を大きく上回っている。この２つを
除けばプラス寄与となっており、企業物価の下落が一部に集中していたことが分かる。 
実際、プラスチック製品が前月比＋1.1％、金属製品が同＋0.7％、化学製品が同＋0.3％、飲食
料品が同＋0.2％、電子部品・デバイスが同＋0.4％となるなど、多くの工業製品で価格上昇がみ
られた。また、報道によれば、日銀が公表する515 品目のうち、前年比で価格が上昇した品目は
460 品目と、全体の約９割に達している。国内企業物価の前年比上昇率は鈍化したものの、値上が
りは依然として幅広い品目に及んでいる。企業段階での物価上昇圧力はなお根強いとみるべきだ
ろう。

2. 川中価格は下落も、川下への価格転嫁は続く 
石油化学関連では、春先にみられた急激な価格上昇の反動が続いている。石油・石炭製品は前
月比▲2.2％と、３ヵ月連続で下落した。ベンゼンが同＋17.2％、キシレンが同＋6.0％と、川上
の石油化学系基礎製品の一部で上昇もみられるが、ベンゼンについては中国の製油所の定期修理
に伴う供給減少という個別要因も影響しているようだ。川上価格全体でみれば、このところやや
落ち着きもみられる。 
川中に位置する合成樹脂は前月比▲0.8％と、８月の同▲2.7％に続いて下落した。内訳をみて
も、ポリエチレンが同▲2.9％、ポリプロピレンが同▲0.2％となるなど、主要品目で下落が続い
ている。４月の川上価格急騰を受けて５～６月には合成樹脂への価格波及が進んだが、夏場以降
は反落が続いている。川中段階での追加的なコスト上昇圧力は弱まりつつあると言えそうだ。 
これに対して、川下価格の動きは対照的である。プラスチック製品は９月も前月比＋1.1％と上
（出所）日本銀行「企業物価指数」

### Page 3

3/4 
昇し、前年比では＋12.8％と８月の＋11.3％からさらに伸びを高めた。川中の合成樹脂価格が２
ヵ月連続で下落する一方、その先に位置するプラスチック製品では前年比上昇率がむしろ加速し
ており、川中と川下の価格動向の乖離が一段と鮮明になった。 
品目別にみても、価格上昇は最終製品に近い段階で続いている。９月はプラスチック製容器
（中空成形を除く）が前月比＋11.2％と大幅に上昇するなど、容器類を中心に価格上昇が続い
た。こうした包装資材は食品や日用品など幅広い商品に用いられるため、川下企業のコスト負担
を高める要因となる。 
こうした川中と川下の価格動向の違いの背景には、企業間の価格転嫁に時間差があることが挙
げられる。原材料価格が低下しても、川下企業の仕入れコストが直ちに下がるわけではない。高
値で調達した原材料の在庫が残っているほか、取引価格の改定も一定の間隔で行われるためであ
る。加えて、物流費や人件費など原材料以外のコスト上昇も続いている。９月の「合成樹脂の下
落とプラスチック製品の上昇」という組み合わせは、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が川下でなお続
いていることを示すものといえる。 
また、今回の企業物価では、ＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景とする価格上昇も注目される。９月の
輸出物価指数は契約通貨ベースで前月比＋1.4％となったが、このうち電気・電子機器の寄与度は
＋0.80％ポイントと、上昇の半分以上を占めた。輸入物価でもモス型メモリ集積回路や銅鉱など
が上昇しており、世界的なＡＩ・データセンター需要の拡大による影響がうかがわれる。ＡＩ関
連需要の拡大は日本の輸出や企業収益にとって追い風となる一方、半導体や銅などの調達コスト
上昇を通じて、国内の関連製品価格を押し上げる側面もある。資源価格とは異なる経路からの物
価上昇圧力として、今後の動向が注目される。

3. 川下転嫁と資源高で、物価上昇圧力は長期化へ 
先行きについては、既往のコスト高の川下への価格転嫁が今後もしばらく続く可能性が高い。
川中の合成樹脂価格が下落する一方、川下のプラスチック製品では値上がりが続いており、原材
料価格の低下が川下価格の押し下げにつながるまでには、なお時間を要するとみられる。物流費
や人件費などを含めた企業のコスト水準も依然として高く、価格転嫁の動きは簡単には収まらな
いだろう。 
加えて、資源価格の再上昇による新たなコスト上昇圧力にも注意が必要だ。９月の輸入物価指
数は円ベースでは前月比▲0.2％と小幅な下落となったものの、契約通貨ベースでは同＋1.0％と
上昇に転じた。なかでも石油・石炭・天然ガスは契約通貨ベースで同＋2.3％、円ベースでも同＋
0.9％と上昇している。資源価格再上昇の影響がラグをもって輸入物価押し上げにつながること
で、10 月の輸入物価は上昇に転じる可能性が高まっている。こうした動きが国内の石油・化学関
連価格に時間差を伴って波及すれば、川上からの物価上昇圧力が再び強まることになる。 
このため、国内企業物価の前年比上昇率は９月に＋６％台へ鈍化したものの、今後も当面＋
６％台を中心とした高い伸びが続く可能性が高い。農林水産物の価格下落は引き続き企業物価の
押し下げ要因となる可能性がある一方、既往のコスト高の川下転嫁が続くなか、資源価格や為替

### Page 4

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
4/4
を通じた新たなコスト上昇が重なれば、企業物価の明確な鈍化には時間を要するだろう。
消費者物価への影響にも注意が必要である。米価格の下落は食料品価格を押し下げる要因とな
るものの、米以外の食料品では上昇圧力が強まる可能性が高い。プラスチック製容器など包装資
材の価格上昇が続いているほか、物流費や人件費なども高止まりしており、こうしたコスト増が
時間差を伴って最終販売価格へ転嫁されるとみられる。米価下落が一定の下押し要因となるなか
でも、食料品全体では年度後半にかけて価格上昇圧力が強まると予想している。
総じてみれば、９月の企業物価は、農林水産物や石油製品の下落によって全体の上昇率が鈍化
したものの、川下への価格転嫁はなお続き、ＡＩ関連の価格上昇も目立つ結果となった。先行き

は、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が続くなか、資源価格の高止まりによって川上からの上昇圧力が
再び強まるリスクもある。企業物価は当面高い伸びが続く可能性が高いだろう。
消費者物価についても、既往のコスト高の価格転嫁が続くことで、上昇圧力が強まるとみられ
る。筆者は、26 年末から27 年初にかけて消費者物価上昇率が前年比＋３％を上回ると予想してお
り、今回の企業物価指数もこうした見通しと整合的な結果だったと考えている。当面、物価の上
振れに警戒が必要な局面が続くだろう。

## Document 514: 宏观专题报告：能源价格走高支撑通胀延续回升

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观专题报告：能源价格走高支撑通胀延续回升.pdf
Report source: 国贸期货
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31 号

能源价格走高支撑通胀延续回升 
投资观点：

宏观 
报告日期 2026-10-14 专题报告 
   （1）国家统计局10 月14 日公布的数据显示，1）CPI 同比+0.9%，
前值+0.8%，市场预期+1.0%；CPI 环比+0.2%，前值+0.4%；2）核
心 CPI 同比+1.0%，前值+1.0%；3）PPI 同比+5.0%，前值+3.8%，
市场预期+4.5%；PPI 环比+1.2%，前值+0.4%。 
 （2）CPI 同比增速温和回升。受能源价格及部分工业消费品（通
信工具、电子消费品）价格上涨拉动， 9 月份 CPI 环比上涨
0.2%、同比涨幅较上月扩大 0.1 个百分点至 0.9%；但食品价格同
比仍下降 1.7%，对整体形成拖累，扣除食品和能源的核心 CPI 同
比持平于 1.0%，总指数延续温和回升、结构分化的态势。展望未
来，通胀支撑仍主要源于 "能源价格低基数后的修复与部分商品
涨价"，而食品端（猪肉同比仍降 10.0%）拖累渐缓、核心通胀保
持韧性，年内 CPI 有望在0.5%—1.0%区间温和波动，向供需再平
衡驱动的修复通道过渡。 
 （3）PPI 同比涨幅显著扩大。 受国际大宗商品价格（原油、煤
炭、铜等）共振上行及国内供给端约束的传导，9 月份 PPI 环比
连续第二个月正增长，同比涨幅较上月扩大 1.2 个百分点至
5.0%，为近年高位。展望未来，原油与大宗商品在低基数 与地缘
扰动下短期或维持强势，9 月 PPI 同比或已是阶段性高点附近；
但终端需求偏弱、下游利润受挤压的状况尚未根本扭转，且去年
同期基数将逐步抬升，预计四季度 PPI 同比或高位震荡，进一步
上行的空间有限，呈“冲高回落、中枢抬升”的走势。通胀的上
下游分化短期内仍将持续。
